I’ll start with the trucks, then somehow I’m talking about Thursday, or the church grill, or my boss’s tone… and people just look at me like they can’t follow.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what goes through your mind when your words get mixed up? Do your thoughts feel disorganized or jump around a lot?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they do jump around. I’ll be trying to explain one thing, like the inventory numbers, and then I’m suddenly on the trucks, or Thursday, or the church grill, or how my boss sounded. It makes sense in my head for a second, but then people look confused.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things that others don’t seem to experience, like hearing voices when no one is around or believing something is happening that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really. I mean, I don’t think I hear things like that. People just don’t always understand what I’m trying to say, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly that others didn’t seem to understand or agree with, even when they tried to tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… people disagree with me at work sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I can’t think of anything like that, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was true, even when others gave you clear reasons to think it wasn’t, and you still couldn’t let go of that belief?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I can be stubborn about things sometimes, like anybody, but if someone shows me I’m wrong, I usually try to drop it. Nothing comes to mind like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when people gave you clear proof that it wasn’t?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. If somebody really had proof, I’d usually listen. I can’t think of a time like that.\n\nDoctor: As we're talking right now, do you feel like your thoughts are staying on track, or do you notice them drifting off in a way that's hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it drifts. Like when I’m trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, it starts out normal, then I’m talking about trucks, then Thursdays, then… something from church, or my boss’s tone. I don’t always mean to jump around, but people look at me like they’re not following.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief that something was happening or was true, even when people showed you clear evidence that it wasn’t, and you still couldn’t shake the feeling it was true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really. I mean, I’ve felt strongly about things before, sure, but if someone showed me clear evidence, I don’t think I’d keep holding onto it. Not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: As you're talking right now, do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around or losing their logical connection before you finish your sentences?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then it kind of jumps—trucks, Thursdays, the church grill, my boss’s tone. I can’t think of a time where I just kept believing it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening to you or around you that others say isn't real, and even after they’ve explained it, you still felt deep down that it was true?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I can’t think of something like that happening to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly, even when others showed you clear proof that it wasn’t true, and you still couldn’t let go of that belief?\n\nPatient: Not really. I can’t think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something was real or happening to you, even after people gave you clear reasons or proof that it wasn’t, and you still couldn’t shake the feeling it was true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so.
